<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We replace a three-dimensional Cha–Khuri–Sakovich hyperboloidal end by an asymptotically flat end while preserving the dominant energy condition and each fixed compact interior. For strictly future-timelike CKS initial-data charge, the replacements lose asymptotically no enclosing area and their ADM masses tend to the invariant Bondi mass. Combining this construction with the companion numerical spacetime Penrose theorem yields the sharp Bondi bound for possibly disconnected weakly future trapped boundaries in this class. Horizon-regular Schwarzschild exteriors attain equality at every positive mass.

- **claim-area-controlled-replacement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-replacement)): Let $(\Omega,g,K)$ be a smooth connected orientable three-dimensional exterior, complete including its nonempty compact smooth boundary, with exactly one end of class <a href="#eq:cks" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cks">[eq:cks]</a>. Suppose DEC holds and $E_B>\|P_B\|$. There is a balanced chart such that, for every sufficiently large $R$, smooth data $(G_R,k_R)$ on the same exterior have the following properties:* 1. *They agree with $(g,K)$ on the compact side of $r=R$ and satisfy DEC.* 2. *For numbers $0\leq\varepsilon_R<1$ with $\varepsilon_R\to0$, $G_R\geq(1-\varepsilon_R)g$ everywhere on $\Omega$.* 3. *The end is asymptotically flat, with $G_R-\delta=O_j(r^{-1})$ for every fixed finite $j$, and $k_R=0$ sufficiently far out. The constraints are integrable and its charges satisfy $P_R=0$, $E_R=m_B+\eta_R$, where $0\leq\eta_R\leq2R^{-1/2}$.* *The new exterior is complete and $A_{\min,G_R}(S)\geq(1-\varepsilon_R)A_{\min,g}(S)$. No condition on the boundary expansion is needed.
  - Explanation: A hyperboloidal end of the stated class can be swapped for an asymptotically flat end. The compact interior is unchanged, the dominant energy condition is kept, a global lower metric bound holds, and the ADM energy approaches the Bondi mass.
- **claim-sharp-bondi-inequality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-cks-inequality)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#prop:replacement" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:replacement">1</a>, assume $\theta_+(S)\leq0$, with normal into $\Omega$, and $A_{\min,g}(S)>0$. Then $$\label{eq:main} \sqrt{E_B^2-\|P_B\|^2}\geq \sqrt{\frac{A_{\min,g}(S)}{16\pi}}.$$ The boundary may be disconnected. For every $m>0$ there is a horizon-regular Schwarzschild hyperboloidal exterior attaining equality with $m_B=m$ and $A_{\min,g}(S)=16\pi m^2$.
  - Explanation: Applying the numerical inequality to each replacement and letting the replacement radius grow gives the sharp Bondi bound for weakly future trapped, possibly disconnected boundaries. Schwarzschild slices attain equality.
- **claim-first-harmonic-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-obstruction)): If a smooth trace-free symmetric tensor $T$ on the unit sphere satisfies $\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_\sigma T=\mathrm d_{\mathbb S^2}f$, then $\int_{\mathbb S^2}fn^i\,\mathrm dA_\sigma=0$ for $i=1,2,3$. Conversely, every smooth $f$ satisfying these three conditions admits such a tensor.
  - Explanation: A trace-free tensor with prescribed divergence exists on the round sphere exactly when the function has no first-harmonic component.
- **claim-lorentz-balancing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-balance)): Let smooth data $(g,K)$ have an end in the asymptotic class <a href="#eq:cks" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cks">[eq:cks]</a>, with charges defined by <a href="#eq:bondi" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bondi">[eq:bondi]</a>. This class is preserved by the coordinate changes induced by proper orthochronous Lorentz transformations of the background unit hyperboloid. In the pullback convention described below, the charge vector transforms by $$(E_B',P_B')=\mathscr L^{-1}(E_B,P_B).$$ Consequently, if $E_B>\|P_B\|$, there is an end chart in the same class in which $E_B'=m_B$ and $P_B'=0$. In this chart the smooth function $$\label{eq:balanced-aspect} f_*:=\frac{M}{4} \qquad\text{satisfies}\qquad \frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb S^2}f_*\,\mathrm dA_\sigma=m_B, \qquad \int_{\mathbb S^2}f_*n^i\,\mathrm dA_\sigma=0 \quad (i=1,2,3).$$ Large spheres in the new chart still bound compact inner portions of the same exterior.
  - Explanation: Lorentz transformations preserve the CKS class and act on the charge vector. A strictly future-timelike charge can therefore be brought to a rest frame with vanishing momentum.
- **claim-constraint-decomposition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-constraints)): For the smooth data $(G,k)$ in <a href="#eq:foliation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:foliation">[eq:foliation]</a>, assume $u,d>0$ and use the decomposition <a href="#eq:k-decomposition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:k-decomposition">[eq:k-decomposition]</a>. Let $C=8\pi\mu$, $Q=8\pi J(N)$, and $Z=8\pi J\|_{T\mathbb S^2}$. Then $$\label{eq:constraints} \begin{split} C={}&\frac{R_\gamma}{2}-NH-U\Delta_\gamma(U^{-1}) -\frac34H^2-\frac12\|\widehat\chi\|^2 +Lp+\frac14p^2-\frac12\|\tau\|^2-\|\eta\|^2,\\ Q={}&H(L-p/2)-\widehat\chi:\tau-Np +\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_\gamma\eta-2\eta(B_N),\\ Z={}&U(\partial_r\eta-\mathcal L_s\eta)+H\eta +\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_\gamma\tau-\mathrm d_{\mathbb S^2}(L+p/2) +(L\gamma-k_T)(B_N,\cdot). \end{split}$$ In particular, the dominant energy condition is exactly $C\ge\sqrt{Q^2+\|Z\|_\gamma^2}$. Moreover, $L$ cancels from the combination $$\label{eq:mass-identity} \begin{split} C-\frac vuQ={}&\frac{2NF}{ur^2} +\left(\frac{R_\gamma}{2}-\frac1{r^2}\right) +\left(\frac Uu-1\right)\frac{2-6F/r}{r^2} -U\Delta_\gamma(U^{-1})\\ &-\frac12\bigl(\|\widehat\chi\|^2+\|\tau\|^2\bigr) +\frac vu\widehat\chi:\tau-\|\eta\|^2 -\frac vu\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_\gamma\eta-2\eta(B_N)\bigr). \end{split}$$
  - Explanation: The constraint densities are written in leaf variables. The normal component of the second tensor cancels from the mass combination, which isolates the first-derivative mass term.
- **claim-original-asymptotics** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-original-asymptotics)): For smooth data in the class <a href="#eq:cks" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cks">[eq:cks]</a>, expressed in the balanced chart of Lemma <a href="#lem:balance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:balance">4</a>, the original foliation variables satisfy $$\label{eq:original-asymptotics} \begin{aligned} r^{-2}\gamma_0 &=\sigma+\frac{m^g}{r^3}+O_3(r^{-4}), &s_0&=O_3(r^{-5}),\\ d_0&=1-\frac{3\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_\sigma m^g}{4r^3}+O_2(r^{-4}),\\ \frac{U_0}{\sqrt{1+r^2}} &=1-\frac{m^r}{2r^3}+O_2(r^{-4}),\\ \frac{u_0}{\sqrt{1+r^2}} &=1-\frac{m^r/2+(3/4)\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_\sigma m^g}{r^3} +O_2(r^{-4}),\\ \frac{v_0}{r} &=1+\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_\sigma(m^K-m^g)}{2r^3}+O_2(r^{-4}), &F_0&=f_*+O_2(r^{-1}),\\ L_0-1&=O_2(r^{-3}), &r^{-1}\eta_0&=O_2(r^{-3}),\qquad r^{-2}\tau_0=O_2(r^{-3}). \end{aligned}$$ In particular, $d_0,u_0,v_0>0$ sufficiently far out.
  - Explanation: In the balanced chart, the foliation variables of the original data have explicit expansions, and the limit of the mass variable is the balanced aspect.
- **claim-round-leaf-collar** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-collar)): Let $(g,K)$ be smooth data in the balanced class <a href="#eq:cks" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cks">[eq:cks]</a>, satisfying the dominant energy condition. For all sufficiently large $R$, there are smooth data $(G,k)$ on the original manifold up to the sphere $r=3R$ with the following properties. They equal $(g,K)$ on the entire inner side of $r=R$, satisfy the dominant energy condition, and on $R\leq r\leq3R$ satisfy $$\|G-h\|_h\leq C_* r^{-3}.$$ On their entire domain they also satisfy $G\geq(1-C_*R^{-3})g$, after enlarging $C_*$ if necessary. The constants and the threshold for $R$ depend on the given data and fixed cutoff profiles, not on $R$. At $r=3R$ their full jets agree with the prescription $$\begin{gathered} \gamma=r^2\sigma,\qquad s=\eta=\tau=0,\qquad v=r,\qquad F=f_*+c_R(r),\\ L=1+r^{-2},\qquad U=u=\sqrt{1+r^2-2F/r}. \end{gathered}$$ Here $f_*=M/4$ is the balanced mass function, and $c_R$ is smooth, nonnegative, and satisfies $$\label{eq:mass-padding} c_R(r)=\int_R^r w(\rho)\rho^{-3/2}\,\mathrm d\rho, \qquad c_R'(r)=w(r)r^{-3/2},\qquad 0\leq c_R(r)\leq2R^{-1/2}\quad(r\geq R),$$ where $w$ is nondecreasing, vanishes for $r\leq R$, and equals one for $r\geq2R$. In particular $c_R$ extends by zero across $r=R$.
  - Explanation: A collar rounds the leaves, keeps the dominant energy condition by making the constraint increment future causal, and loses only a small factor in the metric comparison.
- **claim-angular-heat-evolution** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-sphere-heat)): Suppose $f_*\in C^\infty(\mathbb S^2)$ has average $m$ and vanishing first spherical harmonic. There are smooth functions $f(t,\omega)$ and trace-free symmetric tensors $\mathcal T(t,\omega)$, for $t\geq0$, such that $$\partial_t f=\Delta_\sigma f,\qquad f(0,\cdot)=f_*,\qquad \frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb S^2}f(t,\cdot)\,\mathrm dA_\sigma=m,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{div}}_\sigma\mathcal T=\mathrm d_{\mathbb S^2}f.$$ Every fixed-order angular and time derivative of $f$ and $\mathcal T$ is bounded uniformly for $t\geq0$. More precisely, for all nonnegative integers $a,b$, $$\\|\nabla_\sigma^a\partial_t^b(f-m)\\|_{L^\infty} +\\|\nabla_\sigma^a\partial_t^b\mathcal T\\|_{L^\infty} \leq C_{a,b}e^{-6t}.$$
  - Explanation: Heat flow on the sphere preserves the average, smooths the other modes with exponential decay, and supplies trace-free tensors whose divergence equals the gradient of the evolved function.
- **claim-outer-bending** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-bending)): Let $f,\mathcal T$ be the heat evolution and trace-free tensors of Lemma <a href="#lem:sphere-heat" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:sphere-heat">8</a>, with initial value the balanced aspect $f_*$. For all sufficiently large $R$, there are smooth radial functions $t:[3R,\infty)\to[0,\infty)$, $\beta:[3R,\infty)\to[0,1]$ and $v:[3R,\infty)\to[0,\infty)$ such that the round data at the outer edge of the collar extend smoothly to $r\geq3R$, satisfy the dominant energy condition, and have the form $$\label{eq:outer-data} \begin{gathered} G=u^{-2}\mathrm dr^2+r^2\sigma,\qquad u^2=1+v^2-\frac{2F}{r},\qquad F=f(t(r),\omega)+c_R(r),\\ k(N,N)=v'+\frac{\beta}{r^2},\qquad k(N,\cdot)\|_{T\mathbb S^2}=0,\qquad k_T=\frac vr\gamma+\tau,\qquad \gamma=r^2\sigma,\\ \tau=\frac{r(v'-v/r)}{1+v^2}\mathcal T(t(r),\omega), \qquad N=u\partial_r. \end{gathered}$$ Here $0\leq v\leq r$, $v=r$ through $6R$, and $v=0$ for $r\geq2R^2$. The tensor $k$ vanishes identically for $r\geq2R^2$. The function $F$ and every fixed-order mixed angular and scaled radial derivative of $F$ are bounded uniformly in $R$ on $r\geq3R$.
  - Explanation: The round collar data extend smoothly outward with the dominant energy condition, with the tangential trace of the second tensor reduced to zero and the second tensor vanishing on the far tail.
- **claim-af-numerical-input** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ass-af)): Let $(X,q,k)$ be a smooth connected orientable three-dimensional exterior, complete including its nonempty compact smooth boundary, with exactly one asymptotically flat coordinate end and compact complement. Suppose that, in Cartesian coordinates, for some $1/2<a<1$, $$q_{ij}-\delta_{ij}=O_6(r^{-a}),\qquad k_{ij}=O_5(r^{-1-a}).$$ Use the constraints <a href="#eq:constraints-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:constraints-definition">[eq:constraints-definition]</a> and ADM normalizations <a href="#eq:adm-energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:adm-energy">[eq:adm-energy]</a>–<a href="#eq:adm-momentum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:adm-momentum">[eq:adm-momentum]</a>. Assume DEC, integrability of $\mu$ and $\|J\|_q$, finite ADM limits, $E>\|P\|$, and $A_{\min,q}(\partial X)>0$. If every boundary component satisfies $\theta_+\leq0$ with normal into $X$, then $$\sqrt{E^2-\|P\|^2}\geq \sqrt{\frac{A_{\min,q}(\partial X)}{16\pi}}.$$ The full cut class is that of <a href="#eq:area-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:area-definition">[eq:area-definition]</a>, including disconnected cuts and partial or whole coincidence with the boundary. No extension across the boundary, outermostness, outer area-minimization or attainment of the infimum is assumed.
  - Explanation: This is the dimension-three asymptotically flat spacetime Penrose inequality from the companion work, stated in the normalization used here. It is the only numerical input for the Bondi bound.

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-replacement",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(\\Omega,g,K)$ be a smooth connected orientable three-dimensional exterior, complete including its nonempty compact smooth boundary, with exactly one end of class <a href=\"#eq:cks\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cks\">[eq:cks]</a>. Suppose DEC holds and $E_B>|P_B|$. There is a balanced chart such that, for every sufficiently large $R$, smooth data $(G_R,k_R)$ on the same exterior have the following properties:*\n\n1.  *They agree with $(g,K)$ on the compact side of $r=R$ and satisfy DEC.*\n\n2.  *For numbers $0\\leq\\varepsilon_R<1$ with $\\varepsilon_R\\to0$, $G_R\\geq(1-\\varepsilon_R)g$ everywhere on $\\Omega$.*\n\n3.  *The end is asymptotically flat, with $G_R-\\delta=O_j(r^{-1})$ for every fixed finite $j$, and $k_R=0$ sufficiently far out. The constraints are integrable and its charges satisfy $P_R=0$, $E_R=m_B+\\eta_R$, where $0\\leq\\eta_R\\leq2R^{-1/2}$.*\n\n*The new exterior is complete and $A_{\\min,G_R}(S)\\geq(1-\\varepsilon_R)A_{\\min,g}(S)$. No condition on the boundary expansion is needed.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.13"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $(\\Omega,g,K)$ be a smooth connected orientable three-dimensional exterior, complete including its nonempty compact smooth boundary, with exactly one end of class <a href=\"#eq:cks\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cks\">[eq:cks]</a>. Suppose DEC holds and $E_B>|P_B|$. The constraints are integrable and its charges satisfy $P_R=0$, $E_R=m_B+\\eta_R$, where $0\\leq\\eta_R\\leq2R^{-1/2}$.*\n\n*The new exterior is complete and $A_{\\min,G_R}(S)\\geq(1-\\varepsilon_R)A_{\\min,g}(S)$. No condition on the boundary expansion is needed.",

        "anchor": "stmt-cor-cks-inequality",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#prop:replacement\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:replacement\">1</a>, assume $\\theta_+(S)\\leq0$, with normal into $\\Omega$, and $A_{\\min,g}(S)>0$. Then $$\\label{eq:main}\n \\sqrt{E_B^2-|P_B|^2}\\geq\n \\sqrt{\\frac{A_{\\min,g}(S)}{16\\pi}}.$$ The boundary may be disconnected.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-balance",
        "exact_quote": "Let smooth data $(g,K)$ have an end in the asymptotic class <a href=\"#eq:cks\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cks\">[eq:cks]</a>, with charges defined by <a href=\"#eq:bondi\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:bondi\">[eq:bondi]</a>. This class is preserved by the coordinate changes induced by proper orthochronous Lorentz transformations of the background unit hyperboloid. In the pullback convention described below, the charge vector transforms by $$(E_B',P_B')=\\mathscr L^{-1}(E_B,P_B).$$ Consequently, if $E_B>|P_B|$, there is an end chart in the same class in which $E_B'=m_B$ and $P_B'=0$. In this chart the smooth function $$\\label{eq:balanced-aspect}\n f_*:=\\frac{M}{4}\n \\qquad\\text{satisfies}\\qquad\n \\frac{1}{4\\pi}\\int_{\\mathbb S^2}f_*\\,\\mathrm dA_\\sigma=m_B,\n \\qquad\n \\int_{\\mathbb S^2}f_*n^i\\,\\mathrm dA_\\sigma=0\n \\quad (i=1,2,3).$$ Large spheres in the new chart still bound compact inner portions of the same exterior.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.13"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-balance",
          "exact_quote": "Let smooth data $(g,K)$ have an end in the asymptotic class <a href=\"#eq:cks\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cks\">[eq:cks]</a>, with charges defined by <a href=\"#eq:bondi\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:bondi\">[eq:bondi]</a>. This class is preserved by the coordinate changes induced by proper orthochronous Lorentz transformations of the background unit hyperboloid. In the pullback convention described below, the charge vector transforms by $$(E_B',P_B')=\\mathscr L^{-1}(E_B,P_B).$$ Consequently, if $E_B>|P_B|$, there is an end chart in the same class in which $E_B'=m_B$ and $P_B'=0$. In this chart the smooth function $$\\label{eq:balanced-aspect}\n f_*:=\\frac{M}{4}\n \\qquad\\text{satisfies}\\qquad\n \\frac{1}{4\\pi}\\int_{\\mathbb S^2}f_*\\,\\mathrm dA_\\sigma=m_B,\n \\qquad\n \\int_{\\mathbb S^2}f_*n^i\\,\\mathrm dA_\\sigma=0\n \\quad (i=1,2,3).$$ Large spheres in the new chart still bound compact inner portions of the same exterior.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.13"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let smooth data $(g,K)$ have an end in the asymptotic class <a href=\"#eq:cks\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cks\">[eq:cks]</a>, with charges defined by <a href=\"#eq:bondi\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:bondi\">[eq:bondi]</a>. This class is preserved by the coordinate changes induced by proper orthochronous Lorentz transformations of the background unit hyperboloid. In the pullback convention described below, the charge vector transforms by $$(E_B',P_B')=\\mathscr L^{-1}(E_B,P_B).$$ Consequently, if $E_B>|P_B|$, there is an end chart in the same class in which $E_B'=m_B$ and $P_B'=0$. In this chart the smooth function $$\\label{eq:balanced-aspect}\n f_*:=\\frac{M}{4}\n \\qquad\\text{satisfies}\\qquad\n \\frac{1}{4\\pi}\\int_{\\mathbb S^2}f_*\\,\\mathrm dA_\\sigma=m_B,\n \\qquad\n \\int_{\\mathbb S^2}f_*n^i\\,\\mathrm dA_\\sigma=0\n \\quad (i=1,2,3).$$ Large spheres in the new chart still bound compact inner portions of the same exterior.",
      "explanation": "Lorentz transformations preserve the CKS class and act on the charge vector. A strictly future-timelike charge can therefore be brought to a rest frame with vanishing momentum.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-balance",
          "exact_quote": "Let smooth data $(g,K)$ have an end in the asymptotic class",
          "source_id": "PM-260.13"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-balance",
          "exact_quote": "if $E_B>|P_B|$",
          "source_id": "PM-260.13"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-lorentz-balancing",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-balance",
          "exact_quote": "there is an end chart in the same class",
          "source_id": "PM-260.13"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-balance"
    },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-constraints",
        "exact_quote": "For the smooth data $(G,k)$ in <a href=\"#eq:foliation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:foliation\">[eq:foliation]</a>, assume $u,d>0$ and use the decomposition <a href=\"#eq:k-decomposition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:k-decomposition\">[eq:k-decomposition]</a>. Let $C=8\\pi\\mu$, $Q=8\\pi J(N)$, and $Z=8\\pi J|_{T\\mathbb S^2}$. Then $$\\label{eq:constraints}\n \\begin{split}\n C={}&\\frac{R_\\gamma}{2}-NH-U\\Delta_\\gamma(U^{-1})\n      -\\frac34H^2-\\frac12|\\widehat\\chi|^2\n      +Lp+\\frac14p^2-\\frac12|\\tau|^2-|\\eta|^2,\\\\\n Q={}&H(L-p/2)-\\widehat\\chi:\\tau-Np\n      +\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_\\gamma\\eta-2\\eta(B_N),\\\\\n Z={}&U(\\partial_r\\eta-\\mathcal L_s\\eta)+H\\eta\n      +\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_\\gamma\\tau-\\mathrm d_{\\mathbb S^2}(L+p/2)\n      +(L\\gamma-k_T)(B_N,\\cdot).\n \\end{split}$$ In particular, the dominant energy condition is exactly $C\\ge\\sqrt{Q^2+|Z|_\\gamma^2}$. Moreover, $L$ cancels from the combination $$\\label{eq:mass-identity}\n \\begin{split}\n C-\\frac vuQ={}&\\frac{2NF}{ur^2}\n  +\\left(\\frac{R_\\gamma}{2}-\\frac1{r^2}\\right)\n  +\\left(\\frac Uu-1\\right)\\frac{2-6F/r}{r^2}\n  -U\\Delta_\\gamma(U^{-1})\\\\\n &-\\frac12\\bigl(|\\widehat\\chi|^2+|\\tau|^2\\bigr)\n  +\\frac vu\\widehat\\chi:\\tau-|\\eta|^2\n  -\\frac vu\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_\\gamma\\eta-2\\eta(B_N)\\bigr).\n \\end{split}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-260.13"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-constraints",
          "exact_quote": "For the smooth data $(G,k)$ in <a href=\"#eq:foliation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:foliation\">[eq:foliation]</a>, assume $u,d>0$ and use the decomposition <a href=\"#eq:k-decomposition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:k-decomposition\">[eq:k-decomposition]</a>. Let $C=8\\pi\\mu$, $Q=8\\pi J(N)$, and $Z=8\\pi J|_{T\\mathbb S^2}$. Then $$\\label{eq:constraints}\n \\begin{split}\n C={}&\\frac{R_\\gamma}{2}-NH-U\\Delta_\\gamma(U^{-1})\n      -\\frac34H^2-\\frac12|\\widehat\\chi|^2\n      +Lp+\\frac14p^2-\\frac12|\\tau|^2-|\\eta|^2,\\\\\n Q={}&H(L-p/2)-\\widehat\\chi:\\tau-Np\n      +\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_\\gamma\\eta-2\\eta(B_N),\\\\\n Z={}&U(\\partial_r\\eta-\\mathcal L_s\\eta)+H\\eta\n      +\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_\\gamma\\tau-\\mathrm d_{\\mathbb S^2}(L+p/2)\n      +(L\\gamma-k_T)(B_N,\\cdot).\n \\end{split}$$ In particular, the dominant energy condition is exactly $C\\ge\\sqrt{Q^2+|Z|_\\gamma^2}$. Moreover, $L$ cancels from the combination $$\\label{eq:mass-identity}\n \\begin{split}\n C-\\frac vuQ={}&\\frac{2NF}{ur^2}\n  +\\left(\\frac{R_\\gamma}{2}-\\frac1{r^2}\\right)\n  +\\left(\\frac Uu-1\\right)\\frac{2-6F/r}{r^2}\n  -U\\Delta_\\gamma(U^{-1})\\\\\n &-\\frac12\\bigl(|\\widehat\\chi|^2+|\\tau|^2\\bigr)\n  +\\frac vu\\widehat\\chi:\\tau-|\\eta|^2\n  -\\frac vu\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_\\gamma\\eta-2\\eta(B_N)\\bigr).\n \\end{split}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-260.13"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For the smooth data $(G,k)$ in <a href=\"#eq:foliation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:foliation\">[eq:foliation]</a>, assume $u,d>0$ and use the decomposition <a href=\"#eq:k-decomposition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:k-decomposition\">[eq:k-decomposition]</a>. Let $C=8\\pi\\mu$, $Q=8\\pi J(N)$, and $Z=8\\pi J|_{T\\mathbb S^2}$. Then $$\\label{eq:constraints}\n \\begin{split}\n C={}&\\frac{R_\\gamma}{2}-NH-U\\Delta_\\gamma(U^{-1})\n      -\\frac34H^2-\\frac12|\\widehat\\chi|^2\n      +Lp+\\frac14p^2-\\frac12|\\tau|^2-|\\eta|^2,\\\\\n Q={}&H(L-p/2)-\\widehat\\chi:\\tau-Np\n      +\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_\\gamma\\eta-2\\eta(B_N),\\\\\n Z={}&U(\\partial_r\\eta-\\mathcal L_s\\eta)+H\\eta\n      +\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_\\gamma\\tau-\\mathrm d_{\\mathbb S^2}(L+p/2)\n      +(L\\gamma-k_T)(B_N,\\cdot).\n \\end{split}$$ In particular, the dominant energy condition is exactly $C\\ge\\sqrt{Q^2+|Z|_\\gamma^2}$. Moreover, $L$ cancels from the combination $$\\label{eq:mass-identity}\n \\begin{split}\n C-\\frac vuQ={}&\\frac{2NF}{ur^2}\n  +\\left(\\frac{R_\\gamma}{2}-\\frac1{r^2}\\right)\n  +\\left(\\frac Uu-1\\right)\\frac{2-6F/r}{r^2}\n  -U\\Delta_\\gamma(U^{-1})\\\\\n &-\\frac12\\bigl(|\\widehat\\chi|^2+|\\tau|^2\\bigr)\n  +\\frac vu\\widehat\\chi:\\tau-|\\eta|^2\n  -\\frac vu\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_\\gamma\\eta-2\\eta(B_N)\\bigr).\n \\end{split}$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-original-asymptotics",
        "exact_quote": "For smooth data in the class <a href=\"#eq:cks\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cks\">[eq:cks]</a>, expressed in the balanced chart of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:balance\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:balance\">4</a>, the original foliation variables satisfy $$\\label{eq:original-asymptotics}\n \\begin{aligned}\n r^{-2}\\gamma_0\n  &=\\sigma+\\frac{m^g}{r^3}+O_3(r^{-4}),\n       &s_0&=O_3(r^{-5}),\\\\\n d_0&=1-\\frac{3\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_\\sigma m^g}{4r^3}+O_2(r^{-4}),\\\\\n \\frac{U_0}{\\sqrt{1+r^2}}\n  &=1-\\frac{m^r}{2r^3}+O_2(r^{-4}),\\\\\n \\frac{u_0}{\\sqrt{1+r^2}}\n  &=1-\\frac{m^r/2+(3/4)\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_\\sigma m^g}{r^3}\n       +O_2(r^{-4}),\\\\\n \\frac{v_0}{r}\n  &=1+\\frac{\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_\\sigma(m^K-m^g)}{2r^3}+O_2(r^{-4}),\n       &F_0&=f_*+O_2(r^{-1}),\\\\\n L_0-1&=O_2(r^{-3}),\n       &r^{-1}\\eta_0&=O_2(r^{-3}),\\qquad\n         r^{-2}\\tau_0=O_2(r^{-3}).\n \\end{aligned}$$ In particular, $d_0,u_0,v_0>0$ sufficiently far out.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.13"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-original-asymptotics",
          "exact_quote": "For smooth data in the class <a href=\"#eq:cks\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cks\">[eq:cks]</a>, expressed in the balanced chart of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:balance\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:balance\">4</a>, the original foliation variables satisfy $$\\label{eq:original-asymptotics}\n \\begin{aligned}\n r^{-2}\\gamma_0\n  &=\\sigma+\\frac{m^g}{r^3}+O_3(r^{-4}),\n       &s_0&=O_3(r^{-5}),\\\\\n d_0&=1-\\frac{3\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_\\sigma m^g}{4r^3}+O_2(r^{-4}),\\\\\n \\frac{U_0}{\\sqrt{1+r^2}}\n  &=1-\\frac{m^r}{2r^3}+O_2(r^{-4}),\\\\\n \\frac{u_0}{\\sqrt{1+r^2}}\n  &=1-\\frac{m^r/2+(3/4)\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_\\sigma m^g}{r^3}\n       +O_2(r^{-4}),\\\\\n \\frac{v_0}{r}\n  &=1+\\frac{\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_\\sigma(m^K-m^g)}{2r^3}+O_2(r^{-4}),\n       &F_0&=f_*+O_2(r^{-1}),\\\\\n L_0-1&=O_2(r^{-3}),\n       &r^{-1}\\eta_0&=O_2(r^{-3}),\\qquad\n         r^{-2}\\tau_0=O_2(r^{-3}).\n \\end{aligned}$$ In particular, $d_0,u_0,v_0>0$ sufficiently far out.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.13"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For smooth data in the class <a href=\"#eq:cks\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cks\">[eq:cks]</a>, expressed in the balanced chart of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:balance\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:balance\">4</a>, the original foliation variables satisfy $$\\label{eq:original-asymptotics}\n \\begin{aligned}\n r^{-2}\\gamma_0\n  &=\\sigma+\\frac{m^g}{r^3}+O_3(r^{-4}),\n       &s_0&=O_3(r^{-5}),\\\\\n d_0&=1-\\frac{3\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_\\sigma m^g}{4r^3}+O_2(r^{-4}),\\\\\n \\frac{U_0}{\\sqrt{1+r^2}}\n  &=1-\\frac{m^r}{2r^3}+O_2(r^{-4}),\\\\\n \\frac{u_0}{\\sqrt{1+r^2}}\n  &=1-\\frac{m^r/2+(3/4)\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_\\sigma m^g}{r^3}\n       +O_2(r^{-4}),\\\\\n \\frac{v_0}{r}\n  &=1+\\frac{\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_\\sigma(m^K-m^g)}{2r^3}+O_2(r^{-4}),\n       &F_0&=f_*+O_2(r^{-1}),\\\\\n L_0-1&=O_2(r^{-3}),\n       &r^{-1}\\eta_0&=O_2(r^{-3}),\\qquad\n         r^{-2}\\tau_0=O_2(r^{-3}).\n \\end{aligned}$$ In particular, $d_0,u_0,v_0>0$ sufficiently far out.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-collar",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(g,K)$ be smooth data in the balanced class <a href=\"#eq:cks\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cks\">[eq:cks]</a>, satisfying the dominant energy condition. For all sufficiently large $R$, there are smooth data $(G,k)$ on the original manifold up to the sphere $r=3R$ with the following properties. They equal $(g,K)$ on the entire inner side of $r=R$, satisfy the dominant energy condition, and on $R\\leq r\\leq3R$ satisfy $$|G-h|_h\\leq C_* r^{-3}.$$ On their entire domain they also satisfy $G\\geq(1-C_*R^{-3})g$, after enlarging $C_*$ if necessary. The constants and the threshold for $R$ depend on the given data and fixed cutoff profiles, not on $R$. At $r=3R$ their full jets agree with the prescription $$\\begin{gathered}\n \\gamma=r^2\\sigma,\\qquad s=\\eta=\\tau=0,\\qquad\n v=r,\\qquad F=f_*+c_R(r),\\\\\n L=1+r^{-2},\\qquad U=u=\\sqrt{1+r^2-2F/r}.\n\\end{gathered}$$ Here $f_*=M/4$ is the balanced mass function, and $c_R$ is smooth, nonnegative, and satisfies $$\\label{eq:mass-padding}\n c_R(r)=\\int_R^r w(\\rho)\\rho^{-3/2}\\,\\mathrm d\\rho,\n \\qquad c_R'(r)=w(r)r^{-3/2},\\qquad\n 0\\leq c_R(r)\\leq2R^{-1/2}\\quad(r\\geq R),$$ where $w$ is nondecreasing, vanishes for $r\\leq R$, and equals one for $r\\geq2R$. In particular $c_R$ extends by zero across $r=R$.",

        "exact_quote": "Suppose $f_*\\in C^\\infty(\\mathbb S^2)$ has average $m$ and vanishing first spherical harmonic. There are smooth functions $f(t,\\omega)$ and trace-free symmetric tensors $\\mathcal T(t,\\omega)$, for $t\\geq0$, such that $$\\partial_t f=\\Delta_\\sigma f,\\qquad f(0,\\cdot)=f_*,\\qquad\n \\frac{1}{4\\pi}\\int_{\\mathbb S^2}f(t,\\cdot)\\,\\mathrm dA_\\sigma=m,\\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_\\sigma\\mathcal T=\\mathrm d_{\\mathbb S^2}f.$$ Every fixed-order angular and time derivative of $f$ and $\\mathcal T$ is bounded uniformly for $t\\geq0$. More precisely, for all nonnegative integers $a,b$, $$\\|\\nabla_\\sigma^a\\partial_t^b(f-m)\\|_{L^\\infty}\n +\\|\\nabla_\\sigma^a\\partial_t^b\\mathcal T\\|_{L^\\infty}\n \\leq C_{a,b}e^{-6t}.$$",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **dominant energy condition** ([source](#eq:constraints-definition)): The dominant energy condition (DEC) is $\mu\geq \|J\|_g$.
- **enclosing cut** ([source](#eq:area-definition)): An *enclosing cut* is $\Gamma=\partial D$, the intrinsic manifold boundary of a connected smooth codimension-zero submanifold-with-boundary $D\subset\Omega$ that is closed in $\Omega$, contains the whole sufficiently distant end, and has manifold interior in $\operatorname{int}\Omega$.
- **unit round metric** ([source](#eq:cks)): Let $\sigma$ be the unit round metric on $\mathbb S^2$.
- **Bondi charge of the initial data** ([source](#eq:bondi)): We call $(E_B,P_B)$ the *Bondi charge of the initial data*.
- **balanced chart** ([source](#eq:bondi)): A chart is *balanced* if $P_B=0$; then $E_B=m_B>0$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-mass-padding-bound | c_R(r) | ≤ | $2R^{-1/2}$ | For $r\geq R$, with large $R$. |
| q-energy-excess | \eta_R | ≤ | $2R^{-1/2}$ | Large $R$. |
| q-schwarzschild-area | A_{\min,g}(S) | = | $16\pi m^2$ | Every $m>0$. |
| q-heat-decay | heat-mode decay bound | ≤ | $C_{a,b}e^{-6t}$ | $t\geq0$. |
| q-metric-error | \varepsilon_R | = | $C_{\mathrm{met}}R^{-3}$ | Large $R$. |
| q-replacement-energy | E_R | = | $m_B+c_R(\infty)$ | Fixed large $R$. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-obstruction** [claim](#stmt-lem-obstruction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-obstruction): Integration by parts against the coordinate functions, then spherical-harmonic inversion. -> first-harmonic obstruction and converse
- **step-balance** [claim](#stmt-lem-balance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-balance): Track the Lorentz change of chart through the expansions and choose the boost matching the charge. -> balanced chart
- **step-constraints** [claim](#stmt-lem-constraints) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-constraints): Ricci and Gauss identities plus momentum computations in a leaf frame. -> constraint decomposition and mass identity
- **step-original-asymptotics** [claim](#stmt-lem-original-asymptotics) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-original-asymptotics): Direct expansion of the original data in foliation variables. -> expansions and limit of the mass variable
- **step-collar** [claim](#stmt-lem-collar) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-collar): Cutoff interpolation, weighted estimates and a future-causal constraint increment. -> round-leaf collar with dominant energy condition
- **step-heat** [claim](#stmt-lem-sphere-heat) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sphere-heat): Spherical-harmonic series for the heat flow and the inverse of the obstruction lemma. -> heat evolution and trace-free tensors with uniform bounds
- **step-bending** [claim](#stmt-lem-bending) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bending): Radial profiles, exact round constraints and a positive energy margin dominating the momentum error. -> outer data with dominant energy condition and vanishing second tensor on the tail
- **step-replacement** [claim](#stmt-prop-replacement) / [proof](#pm-h-15): Compute ADM charges on the flat tail, compare the metrics, and infer area and completeness. -> replacement theorem
- **step-numerical-input** [claim](#stmt-ass-af) / [proof](#sec:companion-input): Imported companion theorem restated in the present normalization. -> asymptotically flat numerical inequality
- **step-bondi-bound** [claim](#stmt-cor-cks-inequality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ass-af): Apply the numerical inequality to a fixed replacement, then let the radius tend to infinity. -> sharp Bondi inequality

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-global-metric-lower-bound**: For every sufficiently large replacement radius, the replacement metric dominates a factor close to one times the original metric on the whole exterior. Hence the minimum enclosing area can shrink at most by that same factor.
- **inv-replacement-energy-excess**: The replacement ends are asymptotically flat with vanishing ADM momentum, and their ADM energy exceeds the invariant Bondi mass by a nonnegative amount bounded by a negative half power of the replacement radius.
- **inv-bondi-penrose-bound**: For a weakly future trapped, possibly disconnected boundary with positive minimum enclosing area, the Lorentz norm of the Bondi charge is bounded below by the square root of the minimum enclosing area over sixteen pi; equality is attained by horizon-regular Schwarzschild exteriors.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-boundary-own-area**: Insert the area of the boundary surface itself in place of the minimum enclosing area when applying the Bondi bound. -> The bound is proved only for the infimum over all enclosing cuts; a spherical example violates it when the apparent horizon's own area is used.
- **anti-non-timelike-charge**: Apply the replacement construction when the initial-data Bondi charge is null or spacelike. -> No balanced chart exists, so the first-harmonic obstruction is not removed and the energy of the replacement does not approach the Lorentz norm.
- **anti-outside-cks-class**: Apply the end replacement to data whose end expansions differ from the stated component rates and derivative orders. -> The collar and bending estimates use exactly these leading rates and a finite number of weighted derivatives; other decay laws are not covered.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are analytic existence and inequality statements about smooth data on a noncompact manifold, with asymptotic remainders of finite differentiated order. The replacement is built from smooth cutoff profiles, heat evolution on the sphere and a limit of the replacement radius. No finite procedure with checkable finite output is stated, so no runnable reference with an independent oracle can decide the claims.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is a continuum theorem about initial data on three-dimensional manifolds. It prescribes no software component, interface or runtime behaviour. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper concerns smooth geometric initial data and a sharp inequality. It has no game or real-time simulation content. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No tool or verification procedure with finite explicit steps is given. The construction is analytic, with cutoffs and heat evolution on the sphere. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The statements are existence and inequality results. The proofs use smooth cutoffs, infinite spherical harmonic series and a limit in R, not a finite procedure with a complexity bound. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No data structure, index or storage layout is introduced. The objects are smooth tensor fields on a manifold. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns circuits, parallel arithmetic or memory hierarchies. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no adversary, secret or cryptographic primitive. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no learning problem, dataset or training objective. The results are deterministic geometric theorems. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The infimum over enclosing cuts defines a geometric quantity, and the paper does not assume it is attained or compute it. No optimisation problem is posed or solved. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There are no processes, messages or consensus aspects in the paper. |
| other | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | The paper offers a geometric input for validating numerical relativity pipelines, and the Schwarzschild examples can serve as exact test fixtures. It does not give an algorithm itself. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-timelike-charge** (hypothesis): The construction needs the initial-data Bondi charge to be strictly future timelike. Only then does a Lorentz change of chart give a rest frame with vanishing momentum. Blocked use: ['Applying the end replacement to data with null or spacelike initial-data charge', 'Expecting the replacement energy to approach the Lorentz norm without a balanced chart']
- **limit-dec-and-cks-class** (hypothesis): The theorem is stated for one end of the CKS class together with the dominant energy condition. Both enter the collar and bending estimates. Blocked use: ['Using the replacement on data that violate the dominant energy condition', 'Using the replacement on ends with other decay laws']
- **limit-large-radius** (edge_case): The replacement properties are asserted only for the replacement radius sufficiently large. No statement is made for a small radius. Blocked use: ['Quoting the energy excess and the metric comparison error at a fixed small radius']
- **limit-enclosing-area-not-boundary-area** (formal_scope): The bound concerns the infimum over enclosing cuts. The paper does not assume the infimum is attained or equal to the boundary's own area. Blocked use: ["Substituting the boundary's own area for the minimum enclosing area in the Bondi bound", 'Assuming a minimizing cut exists']
- **limit-trapping-and-positive-area** (hypothesis): The sharp inequality is proved under weak future trapping of the boundary and positive minimum enclosing area. Blocked use: ['Applying the inequality to boundaries that are not weakly future trapped', 'Applying it when the minimum enclosing area is not positive']
- **limit-imported-numerical-input** (formal_scope): The Bondi bound rests on the companion asymptotically flat inequality, which is imported rather than proved here. Blocked use: ['Treating the Bondi bound as independent of the companion theorem']
- **limit-equality-classification** (unknown): Equality is attained by the Schwarzschild examples, but the argument does not classify equality for general original data. Blocked use: ['Concluding that equality forces the original data to be Schwarzschild']
- **limit-tail-constant-not-uniform** (approximation): The refined tail expansion has a decay constant that may depend on the replacement radius, unlike the uniform bounds and the energy estimates. Blocked use: ['Using the tail remainder constant uniformly in the replacement radius']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 260 (`lean/docs/260.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Area-controlled end replacement and the Bondi–Penrose inequality in the CKS class](../../preprints/Area-controlled-end-replacement-and-the-Bondi-Penrose-inequality-in-the-CKS-class-September-27-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization replaces a three-dimensional Cha–Khuri–Sakovich hyperboloidal end by asymptotically flat ends while preserving each fixed compact interior, completeness, and the dominant energy condition. For strictly future-timelike initial-data charge, the ADM masses approach the invariant Bondi mass and the loss of enclosing area tends to zero.
>
> The Comparator also includes canonical Schwarzschild equality examples at every positive mass, with $m_{\mathrm{Bondi}}=m$ and enclosing area $16\pi m^2$. The paper's general Bondi–Penrose inequality for possibly disconnected weakly trapped boundaries remains unformalized.
>
> The implementation retains a conditional inequality for the connected marginal-boundary case, assuming the asymptotically flat exterior Penrose inequality. This supporting result is not selected as a substitute for the paper's main inequality.

```lean
structure SourceRemainderOn (j q : ℕ) (V : Set OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.Point) (f : OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.Point → ℝ) : Prop where
  regular : ∀ y ∈ V, ContDiffAt ℝ j f y
  bound : ∃ B : ℝ, 0 ≤ B ∧ ∀ y ∈ V, OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.ScaledComponentBound f j q B y
```

```lean
structure SourceCKSComponents (j qrad : ℕ) (V : Set OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.Point)
    (A : OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.I → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.I → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.Point → ℝ) (mr : OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.Angle → ℝ) (mg : OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.I → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.I → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.Angle → ℝ) : Prop where
  radial : OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.SourceRemainderOn j qrad V (fun y => A 0 0 y-OAI.CKSLorentz.ordinaryLeadingComponent 5 mr y)
  mixed : ∀ i k, (i=0 ∧ k≠0) ∨ (i≠0 ∧ k=0) → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.SourceRemainderOn j 3 V (A i k)
  angular : ∀ i k, i≠0 → k≠0 → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.SourceRemainderOn j 2 V
    (fun y => A i k y-OAI.CKSLorentz.ordinaryLeadingComponent 1 (mg i k) y)
```

structure SmoothAngularPatch where
  chart : OpenPartialHomeomorph OAI.CKSLorentz.Sphere OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.Angle
  param_smooth : ContDiffOn ℝ ∞ (fun θ => (chart.symm θ : OAI.CKSLorentz.E)) chart.target
  extension : OAI.CKSLorentz.E → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.Angle
  domain : Set OAI.CKSLorentz.E
  isOpen_domain : IsOpen domain
  extension_smooth : ContDiffOn ℝ ∞ extension domain
  source_domain : ∀ n ∈ chart.source, (n:OAI.CKSLorentz.E) ∈ domain
  extension_eq : ∀ n ∈ chart.source, extension n = chart n
```

  second : OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.I → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.I → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.Point → ℝ

  mg : OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.I → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.I → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.Angle → ℝ

  mr_smooth : ContDiffOn ℝ ∞ mr region
  mg_smooth : ∀ i k, i≠0 → k≠0 → ContDiffOn ℝ ∞ (mg i k) region
  mK_smooth : ∀ i k, i≠0 → k≠0 → ContDiffOn ℝ ∞ (mK i k) region
  metric_CKS : OAI.CKSLorentz.SourceCKSComponents 3 6 (OAI.CKSLorentz.angularRadialTail radius region) metric mr mg
  second_CKS : OAI.CKSLorentz.SourceCKSComponents 2 5 (OAI.CKSLorentz.angularRadialTail radius region) second (fun _ => 0) mK

structure OuterDomain where
  Carrier : Type u
  topology : TopologicalSpace Carrier
  charts : @ChartedSpace OAI.CKSGeometricCuts.H3 _ Carrier topology
  smooth : @IsManifold ℝ _ OAI.CKSGeometricCuts.E3 _ _ OAI.CKSGeometricCuts.H3 _ OAI.CKSGeometricCuts.I3 (∞ : ℕ∞ω) Carrier topology charts
  inclusion : Carrier → M
  embedding : @IsSmoothEmbedding ℝ _ OAI.CKSGeometricCuts.E3 OAI.CKSGeometricCuts.E3 _ _ _ _ OAI.CKSGeometricCuts.H3 OAI.CKSGeometricCuts.H3 _ _ OAI.CKSGeometricCuts.I3 OAI.CKSGeometricCuts.I3
    Carrier M topology charts _ _ ∞ inclusion
  closed : IsClosed (range inclusion)
  connected : IsConnected (range inclusion)
  contains_distant_end : ∃ K : Set M, IsCompact K ∧ Kᶜ ⊆ range inclusion
  interior_into : inclusion '' (@ModelWithCorners.interior ℝ _ OAI.CKSGeometricCuts.E3 _ _ OAI.CKSGeometricCuts.H3 _ OAI.CKSGeometricCuts.I3
    Carrier topology charts) ⊆ OAI.CKSGeometricCuts.I3.interior M
  compact_boundary : IsCompact (inclusion '' (@ModelWithCorners.boundary ℝ _ OAI.CKSGeometricCuts.E3 _ _ OAI.CKSGeometricCuts.H3 _ OAI.CKSGeometricCuts.I3
    Carrier topology charts))

```lean
structure CoordinateEnd where
  radius : ℝ
  radius_pos : 0 < radius
  domain : Set N
  isOpen : IsOpen domain
  coordinate : N → OAI.CKSLorentz.E
  inverse : OAI.CKSLorentz.E → N
  smooth : ContMDiffOn OAI.CKSGeometricCuts.I3 𝓘(ℝ,OAI.CKSLorentz.E) ∞ coordinate domain
  inverse_smooth : ContMDiffOn 𝓘(ℝ,OAI.CKSLorentz.E) OAI.CKSGeometricCuts.I3 ∞ inverse {y | radius < ‖y‖}
  mapsTo : ∀ x ∈ domain, radius < ‖coordinate x‖
  left_inverse : ∀ x ∈ domain, inverse (coordinate x) = x
  right_inverse : ∀ y, radius < ‖y‖ → inverse y ∈ domain ∧ coordinate (inverse y) = y
  closed_far_side : ∀ r, radius < r → IsClosed {x : N | x ∈ domain ∧ r ≤ ‖coordinate x‖}
  compact_inner_side : ∀ r, radius < r → IsCompact {x : N | x ∉ domain ∨ ‖coordinate x‖ ≤ r}
```

```lean
structure HorizonRegularGraph (m : ℝ) : Prop where
  ambient_smooth : ∀ z : OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.Spacetime, z.2 ≠ 0 → ContDiffAt ℝ ∞ (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.advancedMetric m) z
  ambient_symmetric : ∀ z a b, OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.advancedMetric m z a b = OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.advancedMetric m z b a
  ambient_nondegenerate : ∀ z : OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.Spacetime, z.2 ≠ 0 → ∀ a : OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.Spacetime,
    (∀ b, OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.advancedMetric m z a b = 0) → a = 0
  graph_smooth_across : ∀ x : OAI.CKSBoundarySurface.E3, m < ‖x‖ → ContDiffAt ℝ ∞ (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.advancedGraph m) x
  graph_injective : Function.Injective (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.advancedGraph m)
  graph_derivative : ∀ x : OAI.CKSBoundarySurface.E3, m < ‖x‖ → ∀ a,
    fderiv ℝ (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.advancedGraph m) x a = OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.graphTangent m x a
  horizon_time : OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.advancedTime m (2*m) = 0
  horizon_slope : OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.advancedSlope m (2*m) = 1/2
  induced_metric : ∀ x : OAI.CKSBoundarySurface.E3, 2*m ≤ ‖x‖ → ∀ a b,
    OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.advancedMetric m (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.advancedGraph m x) (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.graphTangent m x a) (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.graphTangent m x b) = OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.cartMetric m x a b
  normal_unit : ∀ x : OAI.CKSBoundarySurface.E3, 2*m ≤ ‖x‖ →
    OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.advancedMetric m (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.advancedGraph m x) (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.futureNormal m (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.advancedGraph m x))
      (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.futureNormal m (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.advancedGraph m x)) = -1
  normal_orthogonal : ∀ x : OAI.CKSBoundarySurface.E3, 2*m ≤ ‖x‖ → ∀ a,
    OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.advancedMetric m (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.advancedGraph m x) (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.futureNormal m (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.advancedGraph m x)) (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.graphTangent m x a) = 0
  future_facing : ∀ x : OAI.CKSBoundarySurface.E3, 2*m ≤ ‖x‖ → 0 < (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.futureNormal m (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.advancedGraph m x)).1
  induced_second_form : ∀ x : OAI.CKSBoundarySurface.E3, 2*m ≤ ‖x‖ → ∀ a b,
    OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.covariantPair (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.advancedMetric m) (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.futureNormal m) (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.advancedGraph m x)
      (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.graphTangent m x a) (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.graphTangent m x b) = OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.cartTensor m x a b
```

      "kind": "structure",
      "name": "SourceRemainderOn",
      "statement_lean": "structure SourceRemainderOn (j q : ℕ) (V : Set OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.Point) (f : OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.Point → ℝ) : Prop where\n  regular : ∀ y ∈ V, ContDiffAt ℝ j f y\n  bound : ∃ B : ℝ, 0 ≤ B ∧ ∀ y ∈ V, OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.ScaledComponentBound f j q B y"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "structure",
      "name": "SourceCKSComponents",
      "statement_lean": "structure SourceCKSComponents (j qrad : ℕ) (V : Set OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.Point)\n    (A : OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.I → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.I → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.Point → ℝ) (mr : OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.Angle → ℝ) (mg : OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.I → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.I → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.Angle → ℝ) : Prop where\n  radial : OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.SourceRemainderOn j qrad V (fun y => A 0 0 y-OAI.CKSLorentz.ordinaryLeadingComponent 5 mr y)\n  mixed : ∀ i k, (i=0 ∧ k≠0) ∨ (i≠0 ∧ k=0) → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.SourceRemainderOn j 3 V (A i k)\n  angular : ∀ i k, i≠0 → k≠0 → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.SourceRemainderOn j 2 V\n    (fun y => A i k y-OAI.CKSLorentz.ordinaryLeadingComponent 1 (mg i k) y)"
    },

      "statement_lean": "structure SmoothAngularPatch where\n  chart : OpenPartialHomeomorph OAI.CKSLorentz.Sphere OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.Angle\n  param_smooth : ContDiffOn ℝ ∞ (fun θ => (chart.symm θ : OAI.CKSLorentz.E)) chart.target\n  extension : OAI.CKSLorentz.E → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.Angle\n  domain : Set OAI.CKSLorentz.E\n  isOpen_domain : IsOpen domain\n  extension_smooth : ContDiffOn ℝ ∞ extension domain\n  source_domain : ∀ n ∈ chart.source, (n:OAI.CKSLorentz.E) ∈ domain\n  extension_eq : ∀ n ∈ chart.source, extension n = chart n"

      "statement_lean": "structure CKSTensorPatch where\n  patch : OAI.CKSLorentz.SmoothAngularPatch\n  region : Set OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.Angle\n  open_region : IsOpen region\n  region_target : region ⊆ patch.chart.target\n  radius : ℝ\n  metric : OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.I → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.I → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.Point → ℝ\n  second : OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.I → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.I → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.Point → ℝ\n  mr : OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.Angle → ℝ\n  mg : OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.I → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.I → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.Angle → ℝ\n  mK : OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.I → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.I → OAI.CKSMixedGeometry.Angle → ℝ\n  mr_smooth : ContDiffOn ℝ ∞ mr region\n  mg_smooth : ∀ i k, i≠0 → k≠0 → ContDiffOn ℝ ∞ (mg i k) region\n  mK_smooth : ∀ i k, i≠0 → k≠0 → ContDiffOn ℝ ∞ (mK i k) region\n  metric_CKS : OAI.CKSLorentz.SourceCKSComponents 3 6 (OAI.CKSLorentz.angularRadialTail radius region) metric mr mg\n  second_CKS : OAI.CKSLorentz.SourceCKSComponents 2 5 (OAI.CKSLorentz.angularRadialTail radius region) second (fun _ => 0) mK"

m x) (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.futureNormal m (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.advancedGraph m x)) (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.graphTangent m x a) = 0\n  future_facing : ∀ x : OAI.CKSBoundarySurface.E3, 2*m ≤ ‖x‖ → 0 < (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.futureNormal m (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.advancedGraph m x)).1\n  induced_second_form : ∀ x : OAI.CKSBoundarySurface.E3, 2*m ≤ ‖x‖ → ∀ a b,\n    OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.covariantPair (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.advancedMetric m) (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.futureNormal m) (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.advancedGraph m x)\n      (OAI.CKSSchwarzschild.graphTangent m x a) <a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We replace a three-dimensional Cha–Khuri–Sakovich hyperboloidal end by an asymptotically flat end while preserving the dominant energy condition and each fixed compact interior. For strictly future-timelike CKS initial-data charge, the replacements lose asymptotically no enclosing area and their ADM masses tend to the invariant Bondi mass. Combining this construction with the companion numerical spacetime Penrose theorem yields the sharp Bondi bound for possibly disconnected weakly future trapped boundaries in this class. Horizon-regular Schwarzschild exteriors attain equality at every positive mass.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
