The profiles now supply a smooth metric and tensor with the required end behavior. We must verify their dominant energy condition throughout the transition, before using the far end to compute mass.

<a id="eq:round-constraints"></a>
*The exact constraints.* Here $d=1$, $\widehat\chi=0$, $s=0$, $\eta=0$, and $U=u$. Substituting in <a href="#eq:constraints" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:constraints">[eq:constraints]</a> and <a href="#eq:mass-identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mass-identity">[eq:mass-identity]</a> gives $$\label{eq:round-constraints}
 \begin{split}
 Q&=\frac{2u\beta}{r^3},\\
 C&=\frac vu Q+\frac{2\partial_rF}{r^2}
           -u\Delta_\gamma(u^{-1})-\frac12|\tau|_\gamma^2,\\
 Z&=\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_\gamma\tau+(L-v/r)\mathrm d_{\mathbb S^2}\log u
                       -\tau(B_N,\cdot),
 \qquad B_N=\nabla^\gamma\log u.
 \end{split}$$ The first formula follows as well directly from $H=2u/r$, $p=2v/r$, and $N=u\partial_r$; the terms involving $v'$ cancel. These are the constraint quantities of the new data, not prescribed matter fields.

<a id="eq:tau-acceleration-bounds"></a>
<a id="eq:energy-margin"></a>
For the energy equation, direct angular differentiation yields <a id="eq:laplacian-cancellation"></a>$$\label{eq:laplacian-cancellation}
 \begin{split}
 u\Delta_\gamma(u^{-1})
   &=\frac{\Delta_\sigma F}{r^3u^2}
      +\frac{3|\nabla^\sigma F|_\sigma^2}{r^4u^4},\\
 \frac{2\partial_rF}{r^2}
   &=\frac{\zeta\Delta_\sigma F}{r^3A}+\frac{2}{r^{7/2}}.
 \end{split}$$ The Laplacian coefficients cancel to the required order. Indeed, $$\frac\zeta A-\frac1{u^2}
   =\frac{\zeta-1}{A}+\left(\frac1A-\frac1{u^2}\right).$$ The first term is supported where $v=r$ and thus is $O(r^{-2})$; the second is $O(r^{-1}A^{-2})$ by <a href="#eq:lapse-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lapse-comparison">[eq:lapse-comparison]</a>. The angular derivatives of $F$ are uniformly bounded. Moreover <a href="#eq:profile-derivatives" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:profile-derivatives">[eq:profile-derivatives]</a> gives $$\label{eq:tau-acceleration-bounds}
 |\tau|_\gamma\leq\frac{C_*}{r^2\sqrt A},\qquad
 |B_N|_\gamma\leq\frac{C_*}{r^2 A},\qquad
 \mathrm d_{\mathbb S^2}\log u=-\frac{\mathrm d_{\mathbb S^2}F}{ru^2}.$$ Finally, $Q\geq0$. Wherever $\beta\ne0$ we have $v=r$, so $|v/u-1|=O(r^{-2})$ and $Q=O(r^{-2})$. It follows from <a href="#eq:round-constraints" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:round-constraints">[eq:round-constraints]</a>–<a href="#eq:tau-acceleration-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tau-acceleration-bounds">[eq:tau-acceleration-bounds]</a> that $$\label{eq:energy-margin}
 C-Q=\frac{2}{r^{7/2}}+O(r^{-4}).$$ All error constants here are independent of $R$.

<a id="eq:momentum-error"></a>
The energy has acquired a positive margin of order $r^{-7/2}$. To obtain DEC rather than energy positivity alone, the tangential momentum must be smaller than that margin. Its leading term has its own cancellation. Since $\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_\sigma\mathcal T=\mathrm d_{\mathbb S^2}F$, and the leafwise constant factor $r^2$ rescales the inverse leaf metric, one has $$\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_\gamma\tau=\frac{v'-v/r}{rA}\mathrm d_{\mathbb S^2}F.$$ Consequently its exact remaining terms are <a id="eq:gradient-cancellation"></a>$$\label{eq:gradient-cancellation}
 Z=\left[
       \frac{v'-v/r}{r}\left(\frac1A-\frac1{u^2}\right)
       -\frac{\beta}{r^3u^2}
     \right]\mathrm d_{\mathbb S^2}F-\tau(B_N,\cdot).$$ In taking its norm, $|\mathrm d_{\mathbb S^2}F|_\gamma\leq C_*/r$. Also $|v'-v/r|\leq C_*\sqrt A/r$. Equations <a href="#eq:lapse-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lapse-comparison">[eq:lapse-comparison]</a> and <a href="#eq:tau-acceleration-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tau-acceleration-bounds">[eq:tau-acceleration-bounds]</a> therefore give $$\label{eq:momentum-error}
 |Z|_\gamma
 \leq \frac{C_*}{r^4A^{3/2}}+\frac{C_*\beta}{r^4A}
 \leq C_*r^{-4}.$$ For sufficiently large $R$, the positive term in <a href="#eq:energy-margin" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:energy-margin">[eq:energy-margin]</a> dominates both its error and <a href="#eq:momentum-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:momentum-error">[eq:momentum-error]</a>, uniformly for every $r\geq3R$. Thus $$C\geq Q+|Z|_\gamma
       \geq\sqrt{Q^2+|Z|_\gamma^2},$$ which proves the dominant energy condition. The construction used only denominators $A$ and $u$, both bounded away from zero; in particular, the final vanishing of $v$ causes no singularity.

*Differentiated bounds and the far end.* With $D=r\partial_r$, Equation <a href="#eq:profile-derivatives" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:profile-derivatives">[eq:profile-derivatives]</a> gives $D^jA=O_j(A)$ and hence $D^j(A^{-1})=O_j(A^{-1})$. Thus all fixed-order scaled derivatives of $A^{-1}$ are bounded. Equation <a href="#eq:heat-time" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:heat-time">[eq:heat-time]</a> then bounds every positive-order scaled derivative of $t$. All positive-order scaled derivatives of $c_R$ on this region are $O(r^{-1/2})$. The chain rule and Lemma <a href="#lem:sphere-heat" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:sphere-heat">8</a> give the asserted bounds on $F$, including the two differentiated orders needed for the metric’s asymptotically flat decay.

<a id="eq:tail-profile"></a>
For $r\geq2R^2$ one has $v=v'=\beta=0$, so $L=\tau=0$ and $k=0$. On this tail, $$t(r)=\tfrac12\log r+\kappa_R,\qquad
 c_R(r)=c_R(\infty)-2r^{-1/2}.$$ In particular, the exponential estimate of Lemma <a href="#lem:sphere-heat" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:sphere-heat">8</a> gives the useful refinement $$\label{eq:tail-profile}
 F=m_B+c_R(\infty)-2r^{-1/2}+O_R(r^{-3}),$$ where the remainder obeys the same order after any fixed number of angular and scaled radial derivatives. The subscript allows the decay constant in this last refinement to depend on $R$; the uniform bounds and the dominant energy estimates above do not have that dependence. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:transfer"></a>
### Charges, completeness and enclosing area

We now finish Theorem <a href="#prop:replacement" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:replacement">1</a>. All estimates in this section concern the data constructed in Sections <a href="#sec:collar" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:collar">4</a> and <a href="#sec:bending" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:bending">5</a>; in particular their DEC has already been proved. Subscripts $R$ are suppressed when they are not needed.

<a id="pm-h-14"></a>
#### The asymptotically flat output

For each fixed sufficiently large $R$, the outer construction has $v=\beta=0$ beyond a finite radius. Therefore $L=0$, $\tau=0$ and $k=0$ there, and <a id="eq:af-tail"></a>$$\label{eq:af-tail}
 G=(1-2F/r)^{-1}\mathrm dr^2+r^2\sigma.$$ Lemma <a href="#lem:bending" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:bending">9</a> bounds every fixed-order scaled radial and angular derivative of $F$. In Cartesian coordinates $x=rn$ the metric takes the form $$G_{ij}=\delta_{ij}+b\,n_i n_j,
 \qquad b=(1-2F/r)^{-1}-1.$$ Every Cartesian derivative can be expressed as $\partial_{x_i}=r^{-1}(n_iD+X_i)$, where $D=r\partial_r$ and $X_i$ is a smooth tangential field on the unit sphere. Repeatedly applying this identity to $b n_i n_j$ proves $G_{ij}-\delta_{ij}=O_j(r^{-1})$ for every fixed finite $j$. In particular the $O_6$ metric requirement of the numerical input below holds. The round constraint estimates give $C=O(r^{-7/2})$ and $J=0$ on this tail. Since its volume element is comparable to $r^2\mathrm dr\,\mathrm dA_\sigma$, the constraint densities are integrable. The portion inside a fixed tail sphere is compact and smooth, so integrability holds on the whole exterior.

<a id="eq:replacement-charges"></a>
For completeness, the ADM energy calculation allows angular dependence of $b$. Direct differentiation, using $\partial_jn_i=(\delta_{ij}
-n_i n_j)/r$, gives $$(\partial_jG_{ij}-\partial_iG_{jj})n^i=\frac{2b}{r}.$$ The derivatives of $b$ cancel after contraction with $n$; there is no assumption that $F$ is radial. Since $rb=2F+O(r^{-1})$, uniformly on the sphere, <a href="#eq:adm-energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:adm-energy">[eq:adm-energy]</a> gives $$\label{eq:replacement-charges}
 E_R=\lim_{r\to\infty}\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb S^2}F(r,\cdot)\,\mathrm dA_\sigma
 =m_B+c_R(\infty),\qquad P_R=0.$$ The mean of the heat solution is constant, which proves the second equality. The vanishing of $k$ on the tail proves $P_R=0$ directly. The bound in <a href="#eq:mass-padding" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mass-padding">[eq:mass-padding]</a> yields $0\leq c_R(\infty)\leq2R^{-1/2}$. Because $m_B>0$, the constructed charges satisfy the strict inequality $E_R>|P_R|$. These are the charges of one completed replacement, with $R$ fixed. The constant in the refined tail estimate <a href="#eq:tail-profile" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tail-profile">[eq:tail-profile]</a> need not be uniform in $R$.

<a id="pm-h-15"></a>
#### A comparison for every cut

<a id="eq:metric-comparison"></a>
In the balanced chart the original metric satisfies $|g-h|_h\leq C_* r^{-3}$ for $r\geq R$. On $R\leq r\leq3R$, Lemma <a href="#lem:collar" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:collar">7</a> gives the same estimate for $G$. Beyond $3R$, the new angular metric is exactly $r^2\sigma$ and has no shift, while $$u^2=1+v^2-2F/r\leq1+r^2+2\|F\|_\infty/r.$$ Write $M_*\geq\|F\|_\infty$ for a bound independent of $R$. Since $0\leq v\leq r$, comparison of the radial coefficients gives $$u^{-2}\geq\frac{(1+r^2)^{-1}}{1+2M_*r^{-3}}
       \geq(1-2M_*r^{-3})(1+r^2)^{-1}.$$ The angular coefficients already agree with those of $h$. Thus $G\geq(1-2M_*r^{-3})h$ on the whole outer region, while $g\leq(1+C_*r^{-3})h$. Together with the collar comparison and the identity $G=g$ on the compact interior, this gives a constant $C_{\mathrm{met}}$ independent of $R$ such that, with $\varepsilon_R=C_{\mathrm{met}}R^{-3}$, $$\label{eq:metric-comparison}
 G_R\geq(1-\varepsilon_R)g\quad\hbox{on all of }\Omega.$$ We increase $R$ once so that $0\leq\varepsilon_R<1$.

This also proves completeness, including the boundary. A $G_R$-Cauchy sequence is $g$-Cauchy by <a href="#eq:metric-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:metric-comparison">[eq:metric-comparison]</a>, and hence has a limit in $\Omega$. The smooth positive metrics induce the same local topology, so it converges in $G_R$ as well. The manifold, its boundary, and its single end have not changed. Compact truncations remain compact after the balancing chart change because old and new radii are comparable sufficiently far out.

<a id="eq:area-comparison"></a>
On the tangent plane of any smooth cut, the two relative eigenvalues of $G_R$ with respect to $g$ are at least $1-\varepsilon_R$. Its area element is therefore at least $(1-\varepsilon_R)$ times the original area element. The admissible cuts are the same geometric surfaces for the two metrics, with the same filled-side convention. Taking the infimum over *all* such surfaces gives $$\label{eq:area-comparison}
 A_{\min,G_R}(S)\geq(1-\varepsilon_R)A_{\min,g}(S).$$ This includes cuts coinciding with the obstacle $S$. It uses neither existence of a minimizing cut nor any identification of its area with the area of $S$. Equations <a href="#eq:replacement-charges" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:replacement-charges">[eq:replacement-charges]</a>– <a href="#eq:area-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:area-comparison">[eq:area-comparison]</a>, together with the smooth DEC construction, prove Theorem <a href="#prop:replacement" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:replacement">1</a>.

<a id="sec:companion-input"></a>
#### The numerical input and the Bondi bound

The replacements now have the geometric properties needed for the numerical step. We use the dimension-three case of the companion <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[NumericalPenrose](#ref-NumericalPenrose), Definitions 1.1–1.2 and Theorem 1.3], stated here with our physical constraint normalization.

<a id="stmt-ass-af"></a>
<div id="ass:af" class="theorem">

**Theorem 10** (Asymptotically flat numerical input). *Let $(X,q,k)$ be a smooth connected orientable three-dimensional exterior, complete including its nonempty compact smooth boundary, with exactly one asymptotically flat coordinate end and compact complement. Suppose that, in Cartesian coordinates, for some $1/2<a<1$, $$q_{ij}-\delta_{ij}=O_6(r^{-a}),\qquad
 k_{ij}=O_5(r^{-1-a}).$$ Use the constraints <a href="#eq:constraints-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:constraints-definition">[eq:constraints-definition]</a> and ADM normalizations <a href="#eq:adm-energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:adm-energy">[eq:adm-energy]</a>–<a href="#eq:adm-momentum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:adm-momentum">[eq:adm-momentum]</a>. Assume DEC, integrability of $\mu$ and $|J|_q$, finite ADM limits, $E>|P|$, and $A_{\min,q}(\partial X)>0$. If every boundary component satisfies $\theta_+\leq0$ with normal into $X$, then $$\sqrt{E^2-|P|^2}\geq
 \sqrt{\frac{A_{\min,q}(\partial X)}{16\pi}}.$$ The full cut class is that of <a href="#eq:area-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:area-definition">[eq:area-definition]</a>, including disconnected cuts and partial or whole coincidence with the boundary. No extension across the boundary, outermostness, outer area-minimization or attainment of the infimum is assumed.*

</div>

To identify this statement with the cited theorem, its densities satisfy $$2\mu_{\mathrm N}=R+(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}k)^2-|k|^2,\qquad
 J_{\mathrm N}=\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(k-(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}k)q).$$ Thus $\mu_{\mathrm N}=8\pi\mu$ and $J_{\mathrm N}=8\pi J$; the common positive factor preserves DEC and integrability. At dimension three its ADM factors are $[2(n-1)\omega_{n-1}]^{-1}=1/(16\pi)$ and $[(n-1)\omega_{n-1}]^{-1}=1/(8\pi)$, so the charges are unchanged. Its Definition 1.2 takes the full intrinsic boundary of the connected closed end-containing outer domain, including every component and all contact with the original boundary. This is precisely the cut class in <a href="#eq:area-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:area-definition">[eq:area-definition]</a>.

<details id="proof-stmt-ass-af"><summary>Proof of stmt-ass-af</summary>

*Proof of Corollary <a href="#cor:cks-inequality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:cks-inequality">2</a>.* Fix a sufficiently large $R$. The data $(G_R,k_R)$ have the same connected orientable manifold, compact boundary and single end as the original data. Their completeness follows from <a href="#eq:metric-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:metric-comparison">[eq:metric-comparison]</a>. Both tensors agree with $(g,K)$ on a neighborhood of $S$, so its normal, mean curvature, tangential tensor trace and weak future trapping are unchanged. The construction proves DEC and integrable constraint densities. On the completed tail, $G_R-\delta=O_6(r^{-1})$ and $k_R=0$, which give the required differentiated rates with $a=3/4$. Equation <a href="#eq:area-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:area-comparison">[eq:area-comparison]</a> and the assumed positivity of $A_{\min,g}(S)$ give $A_{\min,G_R}(S)>0$. Finally, <a href="#eq:replacement-charges" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:replacement-charges">[eq:replacement-charges]</a> gives finite charges with $E_R=m_B+c_R(\infty)>0=|P_R|$.

Thus every hypothesis of the companion’s numerical theorem, in the form of Theorem <a href="#ass:af" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ass:af">10</a>, holds for this fixed replacement. Using the comparison of all cuts gives <a id="eq:finite-numerical"></a>$$\label{eq:finite-numerical}
 m_B+c_R(\infty)
 \geq\sqrt{\frac{A_{\min,G_R}(S)}{16\pi}}
 \geq\sqrt{\frac{(1-\varepsilon_R)A_{\min,g}(S)}{16\pi}}.$$ Only now let $R\to\infty$, using $0\leq c_R(\infty)\leq2R^{-1/2}$ and $\varepsilon_R\to0$. This proves <a href="#eq:main" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:main">[eq:main]</a>. The examples in Section <a href="#sec:sharpness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:sharpness">7</a> establish sharpness. ◻

</details>

There are two successive infinities in this proof. Radial infinity defines the ADM charges of one completed replacement. The replacement radius tends to infinity only after its numerical inequality has been proved. No convergence of comparison metrics or minimizing cuts is required, and the constant in $O_R(r^{-3})$ need not be uniform in the replacement radius.

<a id="stmt-auto-remark-1"></a>
<div class="remark">

*Remark 11* (The weaker-decay numerical theorem). The companion also proves the same inequality directly under $q-\delta=O_2(r^{-a})$, $k=O_1(r^{-1-a})$, $a>1/2$, for the same smooth one-ended exterior and full cut class, with physical constraint densities, integrable DEC, finite timelike charges, and weakly future trapped boundary <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[NumericalPenrose](#ref-NumericalPenrose), Definition 7.1 and Theorem 7.2]. That theorem does not assume positivity of the enclosing infimum. It gives another numerical input for our replacements; its weaker original-data hypotheses are a separate result, not a consequence of changing the exponent in Theorem <a href="#ass:af" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ass:af">10</a>.

</div>

<a id="sec:conditional"></a>
###### Dependence on the numerical input.

The proof uses the companion only through the displayed asymptotically flat inequality. More generally, this inequality is the sole numerical input needed for the same argument to give the Bondi bound for every weakly future trapped, possibly disconnected boundary under the other hypotheses of Corollary <a href="#cor:cks-inequality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:cks-inequality">2</a>. In particular, it applies when $S$ is connected and marginal, and the open exterior contains no compact smooth embedded two-sided separating union of closed future or past apparent horizons, allowing either sign on each component. These additional horizon conditions are not needed for the transfer. The Schwarzschild examples below satisfy them and attain the bound at every positive mass.

The inequality alone does not classify equality for the original data: the construction need not preserve an equality case at finite $R$. A classification, or an extension to weaker null-infinity expansions, would require a further argument.

<a id="sec:sharpness"></a>
### Schwarzschild equality examples

The radial model gives equality examples once its inner endpoint is placed on the future Schwarzschild horizon. We verify their geometry at that endpoint, their CKS charges at infinity, and the area of every enclosing cut. The examples also exclude every closed interior apparent horizon of either time orientation.

<a id="eq:schwarzschild-data"></a>
Fix $m>0$ and let $\Omega=[2m,\infty)\times\mathbb S^2$. Choose a smooth function $v(r)$ equal to $-1$ near $2m$ and to $r$ for all sufficiently large $r$. Define $$\label{eq:schwarzschild-data}
\begin{gathered}
 u^2=1-\frac{2m}{r}+v^2,\qquad
 G=u^{-2}\mathrm dr^2+r^2\sigma,\qquad N=u\partial_r,\\
 k(N,N)=v',\qquad k(N,\cdot)|_{T\mathbb S^2}=0,
 \qquad k|_{T\mathbb S^2\times T\mathbb S^2}=(v/r)r^2\sigma.
\end{gathered}$$ The function $u$ is strictly positive at the boundary and throughout the exterior, including any interior zero of $v$. Thus these are smooth data up to $S=\{2m\}\times\mathbb S^2$. At infinity $u\sim r$, so the radial length $\int^\infty u^{-1}\mathrm dr$ diverges logarithmically. Together with compactness of finite truncations, this proves completeness. Substitution in <a href="#eq:round-constraints" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:round-constraints">[eq:round-constraints]</a>, or directly in <a href="#eq:constraints" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:constraints">[eq:constraints]</a>, gives $C=Q=Z=0$: here $F=m$, $\eta=\tau=0$, and $L=v'$. In particular the data are vacuum.

<a id="pm-h-19"></a>
#### Spacetime realization and the future boundary

To verify their Schwarzschild interpretation and sign, write $h_m=1-2m/r$. On $r>2m$ the Schwarzschild metric is $$\overline g=-h_m\mathrm dt_s^2+h_m^{-1}\mathrm dr^2+r^2\sigma.$$ Take a graph $t_s=t_s(r)$ with $$t_s'=\frac{v}{h_m u}.$$ Its induced radial metric coefficient is $u^{-2}$. The future unit normal and outward radial unit tangent have $(t_s,r)$-components $$n_{\mathrm{future}}=(u/h_m,v),\qquad e_r=(v/h_m,u).$$ The tangential sphere component of its second fundamental form is $(v/r)r^2\sigma$. The radial component of $\overline\nabla_{e_r}n_{\mathrm{future}}$ is $uv'$: the connection terms from $\overline\Gamma^r_{t_st_s}=h_mh_m'/2$ and $\overline\Gamma^r_{rr}=-h_m'/(2h_m)$ cancel. Orthogonality to the future normal then gives $k(e_r,e_r)=v'$, with mixed component zero. This proves exactly the convention used in <a href="#eq:schwarzschild-data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:schwarzschild-data">[eq:schwarzschild-data]</a>.

In advanced time $w=t_s+\int h_m^{-1}\mathrm dr$, the spacetime metric is regular at the future horizon. Along the graph, $$\frac{\mathrm dw}{\mathrm dr}
 =\frac{u+v}{h_m u}=\frac1{u(u-v)}.$$ The latter expression is smooth at $r=2m$ because $v=-1$ there and $u=1$. Thus the slice extends smoothly to the future horizon, not to the opposite horizon with the wrong expansion sign. At $S$, $$\theta_+(S)=\frac{2(u+v)}r=0.$$

For large $r$ we have $v=r$, $v'=1$, and hence $k=G$. Moreover $$G-h=\left(\frac1{1+r^2-2m/r}-\frac1{1+r^2}\right)\mathrm dr^2
 =\left(\frac{2m}{r^5}+O_3(r^{-7})\right)\mathrm dr^2.$$ The CKS expansion holds with $m^r=2m$ and $m^g=m^K=0$. Consequently $M=4m$, $E_B=m$, $P_B=0$, and $m_B=m>0$.

<a id="pm-h-20"></a>
#### No further horizon and the exact area infimum

Every interior coordinate sphere satisfies <a id="eq:sphere-barrier"></a>$$\label{eq:sphere-barrier}
 H=\frac{2u}{r}>\frac{2|v|}{r}
 =\bigl|\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_{\mathrm{sphere}}k\bigr|,$$ because $u^2-v^2=1-2m/r>0$. This also rules out nonround apparent horizons. Indeed, let $T$ be any compact closed embedded two-sided surface in the interior, and let $x$ be a maximum of $r|_T$. The tangent plane at $x$ is tangent to the coordinate sphere. Orient $T$ locally by the radial outward normal. The surface Hessian identity and $\Delta_T r(x)\leq0$ give $$0\geq\Delta_T r(x)
 =\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_{T_xT}\nabla_G^2r-uH_T(x)
 =u\left(\frac{2u}{r}-H_T(x)\right).$$ Thus $H_T(x)\geq2u/r>|\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_{T_xT}k|$. Reversing the orientation changes $H_T$ only by sign, so neither $\theta_+=0$ nor $\theta_-=0$ can hold on $T$. The argument applies to every component of a separating union of closed surfaces, even if different components are assigned different horizon signs.

Finally let $\pi:\Omega\to S$ be radial projection. Since $r\geq2m$ and the metric is $u^{-2}\mathrm dr^2+r^2\sigma$, this map is length nonincreasing. A radial ray from $S$ to infinity starts on the filled inner side, or on the cut itself, and eventually enters the retained outer domain. It therefore meets every enclosing cut $\Gamma$; at a point of boundary coincidence the initial point is already an intersection. Thus $\pi|_\Gamma$ is surjective. The area formula, with absolute two-dimensional Jacobian at most one, implies $$\operatorname{Area}_G(\Gamma)\geq\operatorname{Area}_G(S)
 =16\pi m^2.$$ As $S$ itself is an allowed cut, $A_{\min,G}(S)=16\pi m^2>0$. Equality in <a href="#eq:main" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:main">[eq:main]</a> follows. These examples prove the sharpness assertion in Corollary <a href="#cor:cks-inequality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:cks-inequality">2</a>, including its connected marginal specialization.

      "caption": "Order of the end replacement, schematically and not to scale. The padding cutoff $w$ is one before rounding starts. The heat cutoff $ζ$ turns on between $4R$ and $5R$, and $β = 0$ from $5R$ on, while $v = r$ through $6R$. The final cutoff of $v$ occurs between $R^{2}$ and $2R^{2}$. Heat evolution and the integrable mass increase continue on the AF tail.",
      "figure_id": "fig:stages",
      "pdf_page": 19,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:stages",
          "exact_quote": "Order of the end replacement, schematically and not to scale.\nThe padding cutoff $w$ is one before rounding starts. The heat cutoff\n$\\zeta$ turns on between $4R$ and $5R$, and $\\beta=0$ from $5R$ on,\nwhile $v=r$ through $6R$. The final cuto",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"

      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[x=1cm,y=1cm,font=\\footnotesize]\n \\fill[black!4] (0,0) rectangle (1.6,1.3);\n \\fill[black!9] (1.6,0) rectangle (3.5,1.3);\n \\fill[black!4] (3.5,0) rectangle (5.4,1.3);\n \\fill[black!9] (5.4,0) rectangle (8.1,1.3);\n \\fill[black!4] (8.1,0) rectangle (10.7,1.3);\n \\fill[black!9] (10.7,0) rectangle (13.2,1.3);\n \\draw[->] (0,0) -- (13.5,0) node[right] {$r$};\n \\foreach \\x/\\lab in {1.6/R,3.5/2R,5.4/3R,8.1/6R,10.7/2R^2}\n  {\\draw (\\x,-0.07)--(\\x,1.3);\\node[below] at (\\x,-0.09) {$\\lab$};}\n \\node[align=center,text width=1.5cm] at (0.8,0.65) {Original\\\\data};\n \\node[align=center,text width=1.8cm] at (2.55,0.65) {Activate\\\\padding};\n \\node[align=center,text width=1.8cm] at (4.45,0.65) {Round\\\\the leaves};\n \\node[align=center,text width=2.5cm] at (6.75,0.65) {Heat on;\\\\$\\beta$ off\\\\($v=r$)};\n \\node[align=center,text width=2.4cm] at (9.4,0.65) {Bend and smoothly\\\\switch off $v$};\n \\node[align=center,text width=2.3cm] at (11.95,0.65) {AF tail\\\\$k=0$};\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "A horizontal schematic timeline/axis with an arrow pointing right labeled $r$. A sequence of adjacent rectangular blocks is arranged horizontally above the axis, separated by vertical boundary lines:\n- Block 1: \"Original data\"\n- Block 2: \"Activate padding\"\n- Block 3: \"Round the leaves\"\n- Block 4: \"Heat on; \\n $\\beta$ off \\n ($v = r$)\"\n- Block 5: \"Bend and \\n smoothly \\n switch off $v$\"\n- Block 6: \"AF tail \\n $k = 0$\"\nTick labels below the axis correspond to the vertical boundaries between the blocks: $R$, $2R$, $3R$, $6R$, and $2R^2$."
